Redone with a real, properly-placed `actorLink:true` token, a real `Combat`/`Combatant`, and real `combat.startCombat()`/`combat.nextRound()` calls (including one rapid-fire pass with no artificial delay) — this surfaced **two real, previously undetected bugs the hook shortcut could never have found**: (1) a read-then-write race in `tickPeriodicEffect` (`config.mjs`) — two `nextRound()` calls fired close together could both read the same stale hp/`ticksRemaining` snapshot and silently drop a tick with zero console errors; fixed by merging the HP and flag writes into one atomic `actor.update()` and adding a per-actor serialization queue (`_queuePerActor`) so overlapping calls always read freshly-resolved state; (2) `#computeSkillModifiers`/`#computeRecoveryModifiers` (`actor-character.mjs`) called `actor.getActiveTokens(true)` without the second `document:true` argument, which returns rendered placeable Token *objects* (`.parent` = the canvas layer) instead of `TokenDocument`s (`.parent` = the Scene) — this crashed `scene?.getFlag is not a function` for **any skill roll or recovery calculation on a character with a token actually visible on the currently-viewed scene**, a crash no prior script-only test could trigger since `getActiveTokens(true)` silently returns `[]` (falling back to `game.scenes.active`) unless a token is really drawn on the scene being viewed. Both fixed and re-verified: rapid-fire real combat rounds now tick correctly every time (no artificial delay needed), and the full chain (3 real combat rounds + 2 more via `advanceTime()` outside combat) drove a test actor to **−4 HP**, confirming the death model still works end-to-end with real canvas tokens. New standing rule added to `CLAUDE.md`'s "Livetestregler": combat-hook/scene-derived mechanics must be tested with real placed tokens and a real `Combat`, never a `Hooks.callAll` shortcut alone.

35. **Wizard attribut-steget: BC-gruppen ett attributvärde ger syns inte, vilket gör "är nästa poäng värd det?" svårt att avgöra (Johan, 2026-08-05).** `DODE.attributeToGroup(value)`/`DODE.groupTable` (config.mjs, ~rad 1121-1131) finns redan och driver CL-beräkningar på annat håll i systemet, men attribut-köpstegen (`#attributeBuyResult`, `character-wizard.mjs`) visar bara värde + BP-kostnad per steg, inte vilken grupp värdet landar i. En spelare ser alltså inte att t.ex. STY 12→13 spelar ingen roll men 13→14 hoppar en hel grupp, förrän efteråt. Johans förslag: visa gruppen inline per attributrad, och/eller lägg BC-tabellen som referens UNDER köpraderna i steget. Textbaserad UI-förbättring, ingen mekanikändring — loggad separat, inte del av det pågående GM-effekter/flerhandsstrid-arbetet.

34. **Wizard step order: `alder` (age) runs AFTER `attribut`/`kapital`/`yrke`, but all three already read `state.ageCategory` (Johan, 2026-08-05).** `#effectiveAttributes()` (`character-wizard.mjs:1047`) folds `CONFIG.DODE.ageAttributeModifiers[ageCategory]` into every attribute total on EVERY render, regardless of which step is showing — so the `attribut` step's live buy-preview (SMI/PSY etc.) is already computed against an age modifier, just silently the default `"Mogen"` (`state.ageCategory`'s initial value) until the player actually reaches the `alder` step later in `ALL_STEPS`. The totals a player buys against during `attribut` therefore shift retroactively the moment they pick a different age afterward — not just a display nicety, an actual "the number you paid for changed" bug. **Found to be broader than just `attribut`:** `kapital`'s `ageCapitalMultiplier` lookup (`:1037`) and `yrke`'s profession-requirement check via `effectiveAttributes` (`:488`) have the identical problem — both already consume `ageCategory` despite `alder` sitting after all three in `ALL_STEPS` (`"...", "ras", "hand", "attribut", "yrke", "magiskola", "yrkesfardigheter", "formagor", "socialt", "kapital", "alder", "fardigheter", "..."`). **Fix:** move `alder` to right after `hand`, before `attribut` — `alder`'s own step doesn't appear to depend on anything from the steps between its old and new position (worth confirming when this is picked up), and EP-budget display (currently computed in the `alder` step per an existing code comment) isn't needed until `fardigheter`, which stays after `alder` either way. Not fixed yet — logged rather than fixed mid-session to avoid context-switching out of the in-progress GM-effects/flerhandsstrid implementation (`docs/dev/GM_EFFEKTFONSTER_ANALYS.md`/backlog 32).

33. **Wizard svärdshand-steget: Ambidextriös's payoff isn't explained anywhere in the UI (Johan, 2026-08-05).** Two gaps, both text-only, in `templates/apps/character-wizard.hbs`'s svärdshand step: (1) the step's up-front hint/tip text (before rolling) never mentions that Ambidextriös's real mechanical payoff is skipping Två vapen training entirely (the 2026-08-04 avsteg, `CLAUDE.md`) — a player rolling for hand type has no idea this is what they're playing for. (2) The post-roll perk text is identical for Dubbelhänt and Ambidextriös (`{{#if swordHand.noOffHandPenalty}}` at `character-wizard.hbs:445-447` — `DODE.hasNoOffHandPenalty()` deliberately returns true for both, see `config.mjs`, since that flag is only about the general off-hand CL penalty they legitimately share) but their COMBAT payoff is not the same: only Ambidextriös also skips Två vapen training. The two results need distinct post-roll copy — Dubbelhänt keeps the current "ingen sämre hand" line; Ambidextriös should additionally state that any known weapons can be combined without training a combo skill first, within the same logical limits every Två vapen combo already has (RP s.59: no two-handed weapons in the pair, and by the same logic no bow/sword-style mismatched pairs — a combo is two wieldable-in-one-hand weapons). Small, text-only fix — explicitly logged as its own backlog item rather than folded into the effects/action-economy plan, since it doesn't touch any of that plan's code paths.

73. ✅ **Systemet saknade en ikon på Foundrys Setup-startsida — KLART 2026-08-18, samma dag som det flaggades.** Johan: *"Såg också att 'Systemet' Drakar och demoner inte har en Ikon på startsidan, bara Game World Ereb Altor. Bägge kan ha samma bakgrundbild."* Bekräftat mot den installerade klienten (`common/packages/base-system.mjs`): `BaseSystem` har ett eget `background`-fält (`StringField`, samma fältnamn som `BaseWorld`s egen `background`) — exakt det Johan gissade rätt på. `system.json` fick `"background": "systems/drakar-och-demoner-expert/assets/backgrounds/Ereb_Altor.png"`, samma fil som redan låg i `assets/backgrounds/` (uppkallad efter världen — troligen redan världens egen bakgrund). Ren manifest-uppgift, ingen kodberoende. ⚠ Manifestfält läses vid serverstart (samma gotcha som `primaryTokenAttribute` redan dokumenterat) — kräver en omstart innan det syns, medvetet INTE gjord samma stund eftersom Johan var mitt i en pågående session.

75. ✅ **NPC:er har inga embedded Items alls — inget att plundra efter en strid — KLART och liveverifierat 2026-08-19.** Johan, 2026-08-18, under granskningen av Anfallsdialog-planen: *"NPC:er borde kunna ha items som blir loot efter fight."* Bekräftat: `actor-npc.mjs` har bara `system.attacks[]` (fritext namn/FV/skada/notering) — inga `vapen`/`utrustning`-items, och NPC-arkets `_prepareContext` bygger ingen gear-lista alls (till skillnad från karaktärsarkets `context.gear`). Ett dött monster har alltså strukturellt ingenting att droppa. Johans egen prioritering: **egen framtida backlogpost, inte del av Anfallsdialog-passet** — den planen byggs mot dagens fritext-modell oförändrad. Öppna designfrågor för den dagen det tas upp: ska NPC-arket få en riktig gear-lista (samma `vapen`/`rustning`/`utrustning`-typer som karaktärer, drag-and-drop redan byggt för de typerna)? Ska loot vara ett särskilt "lootbart"-läge på arket, triggas automatiskt vid 0 KP, eller vara en manuell SL-handling? Ingen av dessa är avgjorda.

   ⚠ **Andra, oberoende beläggningen samma dag:** en live-demo av Anfallsdialogen (1 spelare mot 2 NPC:er) visade att en NPC utan `vapen`/`rustning`-Items INTE kunde parera alls via dialogen — `parryOptions` byggdes bara ur riktiga Items, så en NPC med bara `system.attacks[]` fick aldrig ens ett manuellt FV-fält. Johan: *"Target parries should always be enabled by default"*, följt direkt av *"Thats why i think all NPCs need reall weapon and armour.. otherwise it will be unconsistent."* Punktfixat samma session (se §2:s "Stridsflödets 'smoothness'"-rad — `parryDefaultChecked` gäller nu alltid när parering är mekaniskt möjlig, oavsett Items, med ett syntetiskt `parryItem`+manuellt FV-fält som flyktväg när inget riktigt Item finns, förifyllt med NPC:ns bästa anfalls-FV som gissning) — men Johan har nu påpekat samma underliggande arkitekturgap från TVÅ oberoende håll (loot, parering-konsekvens) samma dag. Stärker fallet för att faktiskt prioritera denna post, inte bara logga den ytterligare en gång.

   ✅ **Löst 2026-08-19, se §2:s "NPC:er får riktiga vapen-/rustnings-Items"-rad.** Johan bekräftade uttryckligen bred omfattning: migrera alla 14 pack-monster, och naturliga attacker (klor, bett, en drakes eldandedräkt) räknas mekaniskt som vapen — bara inte lootbara. Se §2 för hela genomförandet och backlogposterna nedan för det som INTE gjordes i samma pass.

76. **Kodkommentarer får inte innehålla Johans namn.** Johan, 2026-08-19: använd en opersonlig formulering ("Beslut: Project decided ...") i stället för "Johans beslut ..." — mer professionellt eftersom systemet är publikt på GitHub. Nya kommentarer skrivna FRÅN OCH MED 2026-08-19 (se §2:s NPC-items-rad) följer redan detta. **Kvarstår:** en genomgång av BEFINTLIGA kodkommentarer och `CLAUDE.md`/`docs/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md`-rader som redan namnger Johan, för att byta till opersonlig formulering. Inte gjort än — flaggat som nästa prioritet efter NPC-items-passet.

Johan, 2026-08-19. Stor, genomgripande refaktorering (t.ex. `item-vapen.mjs`s `styGroup`/`baseValue`/mallarnas svenska variabelnamn, hela mappstrukturens svenska filnamn i vissa fall) — inte påbörjad. Näst högsta prioritet efter backlogpost 76.

78. ✅ **Besvärjelsers skade-/måltilldelningssystem — ALLA 7 FASER klara och liveverifierade 2026-08-21.** `castSpell()` (documents/actor.mjs) rullar i dag bara ett CL-lyckande/misslyckande-slag och drar PSY; ingen skada rullas, inget mål tilldelas, ingen områdeslogik finns (uttryckligen taggad "fas 6+" i koden sedan tidigare). Upptäckt under Områdeseffekter del 1 (se §2), när Johan frågade om en magikers eldklot mot flera mål — visade sig kräva ett helt nytt delsystem, inte bara en utökning av den redan byggda vapenanfallskoden. Johans egen framtoning: nästa pass, kommer sannolikt återanvända SAMMA "loopa per-mål-upplösning" (`#targetTokens()`/loop-mönstret i attack-dialog.mjs) och "massapplicera status" (gm-effects.mjs:s `applyToTargets`) som redan byggts för vapen. Öppna designfrågor: hur CL beräknas för ett besvärjelseanfall mot en FÖRSVARARE (dagens `castSpell` har ingen motståndarsida alls), om/hur parering ska gälla (troligen aldrig, likt en projektil — men besvärjelser har ingen `category`-motsvarighet i dag), och hur skadeformeln kopplas till en besvärjelse-Item (`item-besvarjelse.mjs` har `spellEffect`/`spellDuration` för buff-ActiveEffects, inget skadefält).

    **Uppföljning 2026-08-21 — ALLA 7 FASER klara samma dag.** Johan gav ett tredelat direktiv efter ett användningsfallsdokument (`docs/dev/MAGI_STRID_ANVANDNINGSFALL.md`, 18 fall) — teknisk design, en referenstabell för alla besvärjelser med stridsstödkolumner, beroende tabeller (Skräcktabellen + en ny Snedtändningstabell), och en skadetyp/resistans-mekanik; vid planeringen lade han till en sjunde fas (Fummeltabellen för vapenstrid) som ett direkt uppföljningspass. Se §2:s "Magi/dryck/stridseffekter — mekanik"-rad för hela genomförandet (schema, `spell.mjs`-motorn, kast-UI+godkännande-hook, Snedtändningstabellen, referenstabellsgeneratorn + en första kuraterad batch, Fummeltabellen). Johan förutsåg uttryckligen innan Fas 2: "we will need to fix stuff from phase 1 as we do phase 2" — stämde flera gånger om (saveAttribute/saveDifficulty/triggersFearTable i Fas 2, en riktig `@{E}d6`-formelbugg hittad och rättad under Fas 5:s kurering). Kvarstående, INTE byggt: full mekanisk tillämpning av tabellernas effekttext (bara dragning/visning, samma nivå som Skräcktabellen redan hade), resten av de ~178 okuraterade besvärjelserna, och de genuina schemaluckor Fas 5 dokumenterade (periodeffekt-borttagning, PSY-vs-PSY, karaktärers `abs` via Items i stället för ett flatt fält, m.fl. — se `docs/dev/BESVARJELSE_REFERENSTABELL.md`). Se backlog 80 (nu klar).

79. **Riktiga områdesmallar på kartan** (rita en cirkel/kon, auto-välj tokens inuti) — Foundry har inbyggt `MeasuredTemplate`-stöd för detta (verifierat att det finns, inte kopplat in). Uttryckligen utanför Områdeseffekter del 1 (§2) — målval sker där manuellt via Foundrys egen targeting (Shift-klick), inte via en ritad mall. Naturlig utökning den dag besvärjelsesystemet (backlog 78) byggs, eftersom en magikers eldklot mer naturligt riktas mot "allt inom en radie" än mot individuellt förvalda tokens.

74. **GM-effektfönstrets skillMod/clMod/recoveryMod-lager ger ingen token-ikon.** Johan frågade (2026-08-18, direkt efter GM-effektfönstrets liveverifiering) om effekter syns som ikoner på spelarnas tokens, "typ fire/frost och andra 'foundry native' identifierade normala RPG-effekter". Svaret är delat: DoDE:s två registrerade `CONFIG.statusEffects`-villkor (`armObrukbar`/`handUpptagen`) syns automatiskt via Token HUD — det är gratis Foundry-mekanik, redan i drift. `game.dode.SceneEffects`/utrustnings-/förmågo-AE:er är RIKTIGA `ActiveEffect`-dokument och FÅR en ikon om anroparen anger `img` i effektdatan. Men GM-effektfönstrets egna `skillMod`/`clMod`/`recoveryMod`-poster (världs-Setting/scen-/aktörsflagga) är ren data, aldrig ett `ActiveEffect`-dokument (embedded `fardighet`-Items kan inte vara AE-mål, se §1/§6) — de påverkar rätt tal i beräkningen men ger NOLL visuell markering på tokenet, och periodiska effekter (gift m.m., eget flagglager) har inte heller någon egen ikon, bara en rad i fönstrets aktörssektion. Inte byggt: en tänkbar lösning vore att GM-effektfönstrets formulär även skapar en "dummy" `ActiveEffect` (ingen `changes[]`, bara för ikon+tooltip) parallellt med dataposten — men det är en ny synkroniseringsyta (två dokument som måste hållas i takt) som inte är värd att bygga förrän ett konkret behov (en spelare som inte ser att hen är förgiftad) faktiskt uppstår vid bordet. Flaggat, inte löst.

81. **Öppna trådar från live-demon 2026-08-22, inte påbörjade:**
    - **Skräcktabellen/Fobitabellen är sourcade från fel bok/upplaga.** Ett bakgrundsforskningspass fann att Spelarboken (Bok III, den faktiska Expert-linjens kärnbok) har SINA EGNA versioner av båda — en betydligt mer detaljerad, öppen-skala Skräcktabell (SB s.9-10, med ett separat modifikationsblock) och en 20-rads/1T20 Fobitabell (SB s.11) — medan de nu byggda tabellerna kommer från `Magi-regelboken s.25` (som forskningspasset inte ens kunde hitta en Skräcktabell på) respektive `0_Magi.pdf s.8` (en icke-Expert-utgåva, bara 10 rader/1T10). Ingen av de två nu byggda tabellerna används dock i den här sessionens innehåll (ingen fruktan/fobi utlöstes i striden), så inte brådskande — men bör rebyggas från SB s.9-11 innan de faktiskt konsumeras skarpt.
    - **Dice So Nice per-rulle-färgsättning (anfall=röd, parering=blå på själva 3D-tärningarna) inte byggd.** Bara chattkortets CSS-baserade vänsterkant-indikator byggdes (garanterat säker); ett försök att gissa DsN:s `roll.options.appearance`-API undveks medvetet efter samma kvälls `showForRoll`-dedupe-misstag — kräver en riktig verifiering av DsN:s faktiska publika API (inte gissning) innan det byggs.
    - **Engelsk lokalisering (`lang/en.json`, ny) täcker bara de FÅ platser som redan använde `{{localize}}`** — merparten av UI:t är hårdkodad svensk text direkt i mallarna (fungerar oavsett språkval, men blir aldrig engelsk även om en spelare väljer engelska). En fullständig i18n-genomgång (extrahera all hårdkodad UI-text till `lang/*.json`) är en stor, separat refaktorering, inte påbörjad.
    - **Plundring/försäljning stödjer bara HELA föremål, inte delmängder av en stack** (t.ex. 8 silvermynt måste plundras/säljas som en enda post — kan inte plundra 3 av 8). Ingen kvantitetsväljare byggd i `loot.mjs`/`sell.mjs`/deras UI.
    - **`handlare`-butiksaktörer får ingen token på kartan automatiskt** — Johans stående instruktion ("per default yes") är antecknad som ARBETSPROCESS (jag ska komma ihåg att placera en token varje gång jag skapar en ny handlare-aktör), inte kodifierad som en automatisk hook. Om det visar sig glömmas bort i praktiken, överväg en `Hooks.on("createActor", ...)`-automation.

80. ✅ **Fummeltabellen för vanlig vapenstrid (anfall + parering) — KLART och liveverifierat 2026-08-21.** Johan, samma dag, tillagt vid granskningen av magisystem-designplanen som ett uttryckligt "att göra direkt efter": *"enda som saknas i planen nu är vanliga fummeltabellen och mekanik för den.. att göra direkt efter."* Visade sig vara FYRA tabeller, inte en — Sköldar/Närstridsvapen/Avståndsvapen/Obeväpnad strid (D&DE III, s.39-41), hittade genom att läsa PDF-sidbilder direkt (rå-OCR-extraktet hade ordmellanslagen helt borttagna). Ny `CONFIG.DODE.rollWeaponFummelTable(tableKey)` (config.mjs) hanterar rad 20:s rekursiva omdragningsregel; `attackFummelTableKey`/`parryFummelTableKey` (attack.mjs) väljer rätt tabell efter vapentyp, sköldparering får sin egen tabell. Draget sker i `resolveAttack()`s redan rena beräkningsfas, aldrig omslaget vid ett SL-godkännande. Fyra egna genererade "witty"-ikoner (Johans uttryckliga önskemål) i stället för neutrala vapensilhuetter, se `docs/dev/ART_STYLE.md`. Se §2:s "Magi/dryck/stridseffekter — mekanik"-rad (Fas 6) för hela liveverifieringen.

### Deferred

17. **RuleProfile metadata layer.** Per-table `ruleMeta` tracking source books and extrapolation status. Revisit when adding content from Alver, Tjuvar och Lönnmördare, or Magikerns Handbok.
18. **Combat refinements.** Attack→damage chaining, shield parry bonus + 1/20 break chance, assassin backstab (no-SB), distance/movement modifiers.
19. **Scene/macro modifier system.** AE-based scene-level effects (e.g., "Dimön PSY ×2"). Requires the universal modifier system. **Analysis done 2026-08-04 (no code yet):** `docs/dev/GM_EFFEKTFONSTER_ANALYS.md`, written in response to Johan's proposal to unify this with the flerhandsstrid design (`STRID_FLERHAND_ANVANDNINGSFALL.md`, backlog 32) behind one GM-facing person/scene/world effects window. Finding: it's one authoring UI but NOT one data store — there are already three independent modifier mechanisms (AE, `skillModifiers`, per-call `MELEE_MODS`), none scene/world-aware, and the effect types needed span four kinds (additive skill bonus, multiplicative skill bonus — "PSY×2" specifically needs this, situational per-roll CL modifier, and boolean conditions/gates like "hand occupied" that have no numeric value at all). Recommends explicit scope: physical injury stays owned by the already-working `hitLocations`/`anatomy.mjs` machinery (not duplicated here), weapon-intrinsic rules like a hypothetical "negates shield parry" stay on the weapon item's own data (not a GM-authored effect), and this window's real territory is GM-improvised temporary conditions with no existing mechanical home (weather, curses, an NPC's hands full). Needs a real Plan-mode session before building, same as the weapon system got.
20. **HP-based hjälteförmågor.** Post-creation mechanic: spend HP to roll 1T20+HP on the hjälteförmåga table. Sheet UI, not wizard. **Source content located 2026-07-27** (previously just "18 entries" placeholder): 35 Hjälteförmågor + 35 Mörka hjälteförmågor already transcribed in the Roll20 project's `docs/extracts/HH_Hjaltarnas_Handbok_extract.md` (lines 218-245 and 671-698), same porting approach as backlog 12 above. Deliberately NOT pulled into this session's scope — a past session (see `memory.md` session 7) conflated this mechanic with the creation-time särskilda förmågor slots (backlog 12); keep them architecturally separate.
21. **Automated Snedtändningstabell.** Magic fumble → currently a chat notice. Could become a RollTable.
22. **CI pipeline.** ESLint + JSON validation on push/PR. Set up when going public.
23. **GM-only character-sheet lock + audit whisper.** Fully designed and verified against the actual installed Foundry build 2026-07-27, not yet implemented (paused in favor of backlog 12/skill-catalog work above, which it depends on being real data first). Scope: race/yrke/ålder/attribute-Bas become GM-only editable (players see disabled fields); skill/ability add-delete becomes GM-only (no EP-cost enforcement — GM grants/removes "as they see fit"); a `createItem` hook whispers a GM-only chat notice when an item is added to a `character` actor (skip when the acting user is GM, skip on wizard bulk-creation via a `dodeSkipAudit` create-option flag). Mechanism: `context.isGM = game.user.isGM` threaded through `actor-character-sheet.mjs` + `item-sheet.mjs` + templates (`{{#unless isGM}}disabled{{/unless}}`), guard clauses on the relevant `#onXxx` handlers, `ChatMessage.getWhisperRecipients("GM")` for the whisper. `createItem` fires once per connected client (guard: `if (userId !== game.user.id) return;` first line).

    **Role architecture — decided 2026-07-27 (Johan), after reviewing Foundry's 5-role model:** use native `game.user.isGM` (= role ≥ Assistant GM) as the single threshold; **Trusted Player is treated exactly as Player** (no middle tier) — so every gated control has exactly two states, locked or unlocked. Rejected alternatives: strict `hasRole(GAMEMASTER, {exact:true})` (diverges from Foundry convention, would surprise anyone used to other systems, and Assistant GM exists precisely to be a co-referee), and a Trusted middle tier that could self-manage skills (doubles the state count per control for no clear table-level need). No world setting for the threshold either — revisit only if an actual table needs it. See §6's "isGM means role ≥ Assistant GM" rule for the underlying verified API facts and the security caveat (this is a UX guardrail, not an enforcement boundary — say so in any player-facing docs).

    **Implementation note:** put the predicates in one place (e.g. `scripts/helpers/permissions.mjs` exporting `canEditCharacteristics()` / `canManageSkills()`) rather than scattering `game.user.isGM` across handlers and templates — same centralisation reasoning that produced `DoDeActiveEffect.isGateOpen()` (§1), so the threshold stays a one-line change.

    **Test user available:** Johan created a `Player11` account manually 2026-07-27 (role 1 = `PLAYER`, `isGM: false`, confirmed via console) in the `Ereb Altor` world — no need to `User.create(...)` one. To live-verify: grant it `OWNER` ownership on a test actor, set a password, join from a second browser session, and confirm locked controls render disabled/hidden there while the GM's own view stays fully interactive.

## 5. GitHub Publication Rules

**What's tracked:** `system.json`, `scripts/`, `templates/`, `lang/`, `styles/`, `assets/`, `packs/` (both LevelDB and `_source/` JSON), `LICENSE`, `README.md`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, and this `docs/` directory.

**What's gitignored:** AI working files at repo root (`CLAUDE.md`, `ACTIVE_TASK.md`, `memory.md`, `PLAN_*.md`) via `/*.md` + `!/README.md`. LevelDB lock/log files. `node_modules/`. `.claude/`. Editor config (`.vscode/`).

**Pre-push checklist:**
1. `README.md` reflects current feature set
2. `system.json` version bumped if this is a release
3. New `packs/*/_source/*.json` files committed alongside their LevelDB
4. No AI working files or OCR extracts included
5. `.gitignore` covers any new file types

## 6. Foundry Development Guidelines

> Distilled from a real bug hunt (2026-07-26, see §1/§2 and `memory.md` session 8): two ActiveEffect bugs shipped and passed `node --check` + static review because they relied on Foundry API assumptions that were never checked against the actual installed Foundry version. This section exists so the next agent doesn't repeat that mistake — Foundry's Document/ActiveEffect internals genuinely change between major versions (v10 → v14), and this project targets "minimum 12, verified against 14" (see `CLAUDE.md`), which is exactly the range where several relevant APIs moved.

### Rule: flag scopes must be `game.system.id`, never an invented namespace

Foundry's `Document#getFlag(scope, key)` / `setFlag` / `unsetFlag` validate `scope` against `ActiveEffect.CHANGE_TYPES`-adjacent logic in `DatabaseBackend#getFlagScopes()`: the only valid values are `"core"`, `"world"`, `game.system.id` (`"drakar-och-demoner-expert"` for this system), or the id of a currently-active module. **There is no mechanism to register a short custom alias like `"dode"`** — using one throws `Flag scope "X" is not valid or not currently active` on every read, always, not just sometimes. This project stored/read flags under `"dode"` for months before this was caught, because:
- The write side (`"flags.dode.source": "age"` in an `update()`/`createEmbeddedDocuments()` call) does **not** validate the scope — Foundry just writes whatever dotted path you give it. Only the *read* side (`getFlag`) validates. So bad data got persisted silently and only failed later, at read time.
- Foundry wraps `prepareDerivedData()` in a try/catch (`Document._safePrepareData`) that logs the error via `Hooks.onError` and continues with stale/partial data instead of crashing the browser tab — so the failure mode looks like "some values seem wrong" or "nothing happened," not an obvious crash.

**Practical rule:** always use `` `flags.${game.system.id}.yourKey` `` (JS) or the literal system id string (compendium JSON, which can't reference `game.system.id`) — never a short alias. When grepping for existing flag usage, search for the literal scope string being used, not just the word "flags", since a wrong-but-plausible-looking scope won't be caught by type checking or `node --check`.

### Rule: verify ActiveEffect override points against the *actual installed* Foundry version, not memory

This system's Foundry install lives at `C:\Program Files\Foundry Virtual Tabletop\resources\app\public\scripts\foundry.mjs` (a single bundled, non-minified-enough-to-grep file — line numbers are stable within a build). Before assuming any Foundry API behavior (an override point, a deprecation, a validation rule), grep this file directly rather than relying on training data or older docs — Foundry ships breaking changes to core Document/ActiveEffect internals across major versions with only a `logCompatibilityWarning` (visible in the browser console, `since`/`until` versions in the message) as a bridge.

Concretely for ActiveEffects in **v14**: overriding the instance method `apply(actor, change)` (the pre-v13 pattern, still shown in a lot of community code/tutorials) **does not work** for changes whose `key` resolves to a real DataModel schema field — which `system.attributes.*.bonus` is, since this system's data model registers it as a proper `NumberField`. `ActiveEffect.applyChange()`'s static pipeline (`foundry.mjs` ~line 50100+) resolves the field directly via `targetDoc.system.getFieldForProperty()` and calls `applyChangeField()`/`CHANGE_TYPES[change.type].handler` — it only falls back to the deprecated `_applyLegacy()`/`apply()` path when the field can't be resolved that way. The correct v14 hook for conditionally gating whether an effect's change applies at all is:

```js
/** @override */
shouldApplyChange(change, {phase} = {}) {
  if (/* your condition to suppress this change */) return false;
  return super.shouldApplyChange(change, {phase});
}
```

`Actor#applyActiveEffects(phase)` calls `effect.shouldApplyChange(change, {phase})` for every change on every effect returned by `Actor#allApplicableEffects()` — which includes both actor-owned effects and `transfer:true` effects on embedded Items. This is also the reason the next rule matters:

### Rule: `Actor#effects` vs `Actor#appliedEffects` — pick the right collection

- **`actor.effects`** — an `EmbeddedCollection` of ActiveEffects owned *directly* by the Actor document. Does **not** include transfer effects living on embedded Items, even though those effects are actively modifying the actor.
- **`actor.appliedEffects`** — a getter that walks `allApplicableEffects()` (actor-owned + every `transfer:true` effect on every embedded Item) and filters to `effect.active`. This is what you want when checking "does this actor currently have an effect from source X applied," regardless of whether it lives on the actor or an item.

In this codebase: race AEs are `transfer:true` effects on the embedded `ras` Item → check `appliedEffects`. Age AEs are created directly on the Actor (`transfer:false`) → `effects` is correct for those. Getting this backwards doesn't throw or warn — it just silently returns the wrong boolean, which is exactly what caused the race-bonus double-counting bug.

### Rule: `.window-content` does not scroll by default — you must opt in

Foundry's core CSS (`less2/applications/applications.less`) sets `.window-content { flex: 1; display: flex; flex-flow: column nowrap; overflow: hidden; }` for **every** ApplicationV2 window, with no exceptions. The `scrollable` array on a `PARTS` entry (`{ template: "...", scrollable: [""] }`) does **not** grant scrolling — despite the name, it only tells Foundry which selectors' `scrollTop`/`scrollLeft` to save and restore across re-renders (see `_preSyncPartState` in `foundry.mjs`). If your app's content can ever exceed the window's fixed height — which a multi-step wizard with variable-length content (skill tables, card grids, equipment lists) absolutely will — you must add your own CSS: give a wrapper around the variable content `overflow-y: auto` and `flex: 1; min-height: 0`, and give any fixed chrome (headers, nav footers) `flex: 0 0 auto`, all inside a `display: flex; flex-direction: column` ancestor. Without this, overflowing content is simply clipped with **no scrollbar and no way to reach it** — this is exactly what happened to the character wizard's nav buttons on the Färdigheter step (fixed 2026-07-26, see "Wizard architecture" above). This system's CSS (`styles/dode.css`) had zero `overflow` rules anywhere before that fix — worth checking any other custom `ApplicationV2` window (not just the wizard) for the same gap before assuming it's fine.

### Rule: `data-action` on a `<select>` never fires — Foundry only binds it to `click`

`ApplicationV2#_attachFrameListeners()` (`client/applications/api/application.mjs`) wires the entire `[data-action]` dispatch system to exactly two events: `this.#element.addEventListener("click", ...)` and `"auxclick"`. There is **no** `"change"` binding anywhere in that pipeline. A `<select data-action="yourAction">` therefore looks completely reasonable — it renders, it's clickable to open the dropdown — but **choosing a new option fires `change`, not `click`, so the registered action handler never runs**, silently, with zero console errors. The form-level `form.addEventListener("change", this._onChangeForm...)` that Foundry ALSO wires up is a separate mechanism entirely (drives `submitOnChange`, calls the app's registered `form.handler`) — it does not dispatch to `data-action` handlers either.

Found 2026-08-06 in `character-wizard.mjs`, in TWO places, one pre-existing and one introduced the same session by copying the (already broken) pattern: `#onToggleHandGranted` (svärdshand's "fick du handen som förmåga" dropdown, written well before this session) had silently never worked since it was added — `target.dataset.value` was always `undefined` because the handler never fired on a real selection, only on the rare case a click event happened to land with the right target; the new `#onSetMotherTongueLang` (this session's modersmål language pickers) had the identical problem. Both were only caught by **actually clicking through the wizard's real UI** — a console-injected `app.state.x = value; app.render()` test (used earlier in this same session, before Johan's explicit correction) never exercises the event binding at all, so it could not have found this class of bug. See `feedback_wizard_click_through_testing` (Claude memory) for the fuller "why console shortcuts hide exactly this."

**Fix pattern, and the one to use for any future `<select>`/free-typed value that needs to update `state`:** don't use `data-action` at all for value-changing form elements. Either (a) give the element `name="state.some.path"` and rely on Foundry's automatic form-data binding (the pattern most of this wizard's plain inputs/selects already use, e.g. `state.ageCategory`), or (b) if the write needs custom logic (derived attributes, array-index writes, cross-field side effects — as both the hand-granted and mother-tongue selects do), register a real `element.addEventListener("change", ...)` inside `_onRender()`, matching the already-working pattern used for the profession free-choice-pool inputs (`[data-slot-index]`, `character-wizard.mjs`). Grep the codebase for `<select data-action` / `<input data-action` / `<textarea data-action` before shipping any new form control — none should exist; the two above are now fixed and templates carry only click-safe attributes (`data-kind`/`data-index`/`class`) for value elements.

### Rule: stop/restart the Foundry server via its own executable — never a bare `node main.js`

Discovered session 10 (2026-07-26): asked to restart Foundry using only Bash (no PowerShell, no `fvtt` CLI), the obvious approach — `node "resources/app/main.js" --dataPath=...` — starts a working server (port 30000 responds, world data loads, `prepareDerivedData()` etc. all fine) but **drops the client into a License Key Activation screen** instead of the normal Setup/world-list page. The installed Electron app (`Foundry Virtual Tabletop.exe`) supplies the license during its own startup sequence; invoking `resources/app/main.js` directly with a bare system Node.js bypasses that, even though the server process itself is otherwise fully functional and `Data/Config/` is untouched.

**Correct stop/restart procedure (Bash, Windows, no PowerShell):**

```bash
# 1. Find the process listening on the Foundry port
netstat -ano | grep ":30000" | grep LISTENING
# -> note the PID in the last column

# 2. Stop it
taskkill //PID <pid> //F

# 3. Restart via the actual Electron executable — NOT node main.js
cd "/c/Program Files/Foundry Virtual Tabletop" && nohup ./"Foundry Virtual Tabletop.exe" > /tmp/foundry.log 2>&1 &
disown

# 4. Poll until it responds
for i in $(seq 1 24); do
  code=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" http://localhost:30000 2>/dev/null)
  [ "$code" != "000" ] && [ -n "$code" ] && echo "up after $((i*5))s" && break
  sleep 5
done
```

No `--dataPath` argument is needed — the Electron app remembers the last-used data path from its own Electron `userData` config, independent of `Data/Config/options.json`. This matches the working directory Windows itself uses for the app's shortcut (`Start in: C:\Program Files\Foundry Virtual Tabletop`).

**Practical rule:** always stop Foundry with `taskkill` on the PID bound to port 30000, and always restart it via `Foundry Virtual Tabletop.exe`, never `node main.js` directly — even though the latter looks like it works (server responds, no console errors on the server side) until you actually load the client and hit the activation wall. This is unrelated to, and in addition to, the pre-existing rule of never running `packs:pack`/`packs:unpack` while the server holds the LevelDB packs open (§ "Kompendiebyggnad" in `ACTIVE_TASK.md`) — both rules apply on every restart cycle: stop fully → pack (if needed) → restart via the `.exe`.

### Rule: `isGM` means "role ≥ Assistant GM", and roles ≠ ownership

Verified against the installed v14 build 2026-07-27 (`foundry.mjs` line refs below), because the naming actively misleads:

- **`User#isGM` is `hasRole(USER_ROLES.ASSISTANT)`** (`:19381`) — i.e. `role >= 3`, so an **Assistant Gamemaster passes every `isGM` check**. `isGM` does *not* mean "is the Gamemaster". `hasRole(role)` is a `>=` comparison unless you pass `{exact: true}` (`:19450`) — that's the only way to gate strictly on role 4.
- Roles (`USER_ROLES`, `:4457`): `NONE:0, PLAYER:1, TRUSTED:2, ASSISTANT:3, GAMEMASTER:4`. Foundry's own defaults use `TRUSTED` for *tool* privileges (`DRAWING_CREATE`, `BROADCAST_AUDIO` — `:4529`/`:4511`), not for authority over other players' rules data. Treat Trusted as a Player for game-rules purposes unless there's a specific reason not to.
- **`USER_PERMISSIONS` is `deepFreeze`'d** (`:4504`) — a system **cannot** register its own entry into the core "Configure User Permissions" dialog. System-specific policy toggles must be a `game.settings.register()` world setting instead.
- **Roles and ownership are orthogonal axes.** Ownership is per-document (`DOCUMENT_OWNERSHIP_LEVELS`: `NONE:0, LIMITED:1, OBSERVER:2, OWNER:3`, `:3694`) and answers "is this actor mine". `Document#testUserPermission` (`:14791`) short-circuits with `if (user.isGM) level = OWNER` — GMs implicitly own everything, so never add a redundant `isGM` check alongside an ownership check.
- `ActorSheetV2#isEditable` (`:38070`) only consults ownership level and pack-locked state. **There is no built-in "GM vs. player-who-owns-this-actor" distinction anywhere in core** — if you need that split, `game.user.isGM` is the correct and only mechanism, not a reinvention of something Foundry already provides.

### Rule: register sheets via `DocumentSheetConfig`, not the deprecated `Actors`/`Items`/`ActorSheet`/`ItemSheet` globals

Found 2026-08-22 — Johan spotted `Actors`/`ActorSheet`/`Items`/`ItemSheet` deprecation warnings in the console (`logCompatibilityWarning`, "Deprecated since Version 13... Backwards-compatible support will be removed in Version 15") and asked whether this system would actually break on a future v15. It would have: the stack traces pointed directly at `dode.mjs`'s own sheet-registration block (`Actors.unregisterSheet`/`Actors.registerSheet`/`Items.unregisterSheet`/`Items.registerSheet`), not third-party noise. Verified against the installed client: `Actors.registerSheet` is now only a thin compatibility shim (`static registerSheet(...args) { DocumentSheetConfig.registerSheet(getDocumentClass(this.documentName), ...args); }`) — the real, permanent API is `foundry.applications.apps.DocumentSheetConfig.registerSheet(documentClass, scope, sheetClass, options)` / `.unregisterSheet(documentClass, scope, sheetClass, {types})`, with the target Document class (`Actor`/`Item`) passed explicitly as the first argument instead of being implied by which global you called it on. The old v1 base sheet classes (`ActorSheet`/`ItemSheet`, needed as the argument to `unregisterSheet` for the core default sheet) are similarly deprecated globals, now at `foundry.appv1.sheets.ActorSheet`/`.ItemSheet`. Fixed by switching to the namespaced calls directly — removes all four warnings, all sheets (character/npc/handlare/all nine item types) reopen identically post-fix. **Practical rule:** any `logCompatibilityWarning` naming an exact future removal version is not cosmetic noise — check whether the stack trace points into this system's own code before assuming it's a module or Foundry-internal concern, and fix it before that version ships rather than after.

### Rule: `ActorSheetV2#_dragDrop` ignores `DEFAULT_OPTIONS.dragDrop` entirely — it hardcodes `dragSelector: ".draggable"`

Found 2026-08-22 (Johan: "row dolk or eld does not seem selectable or dragable") — dragging had **never worked, for anyone, GM included**, on any DoDE actor sheet, despite `DEFAULT_OPTIONS.dragDrop: [{ dragSelector: "[data-item-id]", dropSelector: "form" }]` being declared on both `actor-character-sheet.mjs` and `actor-npc-sheet.mjs`. That config array pattern (`this.options.dragDrop.map(...)`, consumed by a generic `_createDragDropHandlers()`) is real Foundry code — but it belongs to a different base class. `ActorSheetV2`'s own `_dragDrop` getter (`foundry.mjs`) overrides it with a single hardcoded instance:
```js
get _dragDrop() {
  return this.#dragDrop ??= new DragDrop.implementation({
    dragSelector: ".draggable",   // ← literal, ignores options.dragDrop
    permissions: { dragstart: this._canDragStart.bind(this), drop: this._canDragDrop.bind(this) },
    callbacks: { dragstart: this._onDragStart.bind(this), dragover: this._onDragOver.bind(this), drop: this._onDrop.bind(this) }
  });
}
```
Confirmed via direct DOM inspection: rows had `draggable: null` and no bound `ondragstart`, even under a GM session where `_canDragStart` already returned `true`. **Fix:** the draggable class has to be added in the template itself (`class="draggable"` on the `<li>`), not configured via `DEFAULT_OPTIONS.dragDrop` — that array is effectively dead documentation for this sheet base class, left in place only as a comment/warning, not functional config. Applied to `character-sheet.hbs`'s gear rows and `npc-sheet.hbs`'s non-natural gear rows (`{{#if entry.isNatural}}natural-item{{else}}draggable{{/if}}`, preserving the existing natural-item drag exclusion — which had ALSO never functioned for the same reason, so this fix simultaneously made naturals correctly non-draggable for the first time too).

### Rule: `ActorSheetV2#_canDragStart` defaults to `game.user.isGM` — override it for owner-players

Separate from the rule above (both needed together): the base `_canDragStart(selector)` implementation returns `game.user.isGM` unconditionally — a player who *owns* their own actor still can't drag from it unless a subclass overrides this. Found in the same bug hunt; fixed by adding `_canDragStart(selector) { return game.user.isGM || this.actor.isOwner; }` to both `actor-character-sheet.mjs` and `actor-npc-sheet.mjs`. Needed even after the `.draggable` class fix above — without it, `DragDrop#bind()` computes `canDrag = false` for a non-GM owner and never sets the `draggable` attribute at all.

### Rule: scene coordinates ≠ raw background-image pixels — account for `scene.dimensions` padding

A background image's pixel `(0,0)` is **not** scene coordinate `(0,0)` unless the scene has zero padding. Foundry's default `padding:0.25` inflates the canvas around the image — `canvas.scene.dimensions` reports the real offset: for a 1024×1024 image with `padding:0.25` this session saw `{width:1536, height:1536, sceneX:256, sceneY:256, sceneWidth:1024, sceneHeight:1024}`. That means the image's own pixel `(x,y)` is actually at scene coordinate `(x+256, y+256)`. Placing a token by reading the background image directly (e.g. eyeballing where the bar counter is in a screenshot/image editor) and writing that raw pixel pair straight into a `Token`'s `x`/`y` lands the token **256px too far up-left** — outside the room, in the padding void, invisible against the black canvas margin. Found 2026-08-22 placing Lasslo/Bardisk tokens on "Värdshuset — Utkanten"; the fix is to always read `canvas.scene.dimensions` first and, when possible, calibrate against an existing known-good token's coordinates rather than re-deriving from the image.

### Rule: programmatic `TokenDocument#update({x,y})` across a wall is silently reverted — pass `{teleport:true}`

On a scene with walls, calling `token.update({x, y})` to move a token to a position across a wall boundary gets blocked by Foundry's own movement/collision validation — **even for a GM-driven script call**, not just a player drag. The update resolves without throwing, but reading the token's position back afterward shows it snapped to wherever the last valid (non-colliding) step landed, silently ignoring the requested target. There is no console error and no rejected promise — it looks exactly like the write succeeded. **Fix: pass `{teleport:true}` as the second argument** — `await token.update({x, y}, {teleport: true})` — which bypasses movement/collision validation entirely (this is Foundry's own supported escape hatch for exactly this case, e.g. scripted repositioning, not a hack). Found 2026-08-22 repositioning Torvald/Bardisk tokens into "Värdshuset — Utkanten"'s walled room; verified via immediate position readback matching the requested coordinates only after adding `{teleport:true}`.

### Feature: manual GM cleanup of defeated NPC tokens (NOT automatic — reverted same day)

⚠ **Correction, 2026-08-22, same day as the original build.** First built as `Hooks.on("updateScene", ...)`, deleting any `type:"npc"` token with `dead` status on every `active`-flag change — covering both "scene left" and "scene became active" with one hook. Johan caught the problem before it caused real damage: a multi-floor dungeon where the GM switches scenes (floor 1 → floor 2 → back) would wipe floor 1's corpses every time the GM stepped away from it, even when the party fully intended to return and loot or continue the fight. A scene transition says nothing about whether a corpse is actually "done." ("Removing monsters/npc on scene unload is bad... probable need to be SL performed.")

**Current design:** a plain function `clearDefeatedTokens(scene = canvas.scene)` (`scripts/dode.mjs`), triggered ONLY manually by the GM — via a Scene Directory context-menu entry ("Rensa besegrade NPC-token", `Hooks.on("getSceneContextOptions", ...)`, Foundry's standard per-document-directory context-menu hook, `get${documentName}ContextOptions`), visible only when that scene actually has something to clear — or via `game.dode.clearDefeatedTokens()` from console/macro. No automatic trigger at all anymore. Still scoped to `type:"npc"` only (a player's own character is never touched). Live-verified: a real scratch NPC token survived a real scene-activation switch away and back; `game.dode.clearDefeatedTokens()` removed it manually; the real context-menu entry appeared correctly (conditional visibility worked) and the same underlying code path (`li.dataset.entryId` → `game.scenes.get(...)` → deletion) was confirmed working end-to-end.

**Security caveat (design accordingly, and say so in the UI/docs):** role checks, `disabled` attributes, and `preUpdate*` hooks are **UX guardrails, not a security boundary**. The Foundry server validates ownership and schema but never executes system JS, so field-level rules cannot be enforced server-side — a player holding `OWNER` on their actor can set any field from the console. Layer defences by decreasing cost (template `disabled`/hidden → action-handler guard clause → optional `preUpdate*` hook returning `false`), and accept that a determined owner can bypass all three. The only true enforcement would be demoting players to `OBSERVER` and proxying every change through the GM — rejected as disproportionate for this project's home-game use case.

### Rule: any code that reveals a roll's OUTCOME must wait for Dice So Nice first

⚠ **Found and fixed piecemeal across three sessions (2026-07-28, 2026-08-02 ×2) before being written up here as a standing rule** — see the "RÄTTAT"/"Done"-rows in §2 for the individual incidents (grundegenskapsslag, hjältedåd, svärdshand, socialt stånd, startkapital, särskild förmåga, `rollFV`'s EP-streck). Each was the same bug rediscovered in new code because the rule wasn't written down anywhere the next session would read before adding a new roll button.

**The bug shape:** `ChatMessage.create({rolls: [...]})` (or `Roll#toMessage()`) resolves its promise as soon as the message document exists in the database — this has **nothing to do with** Dice So Nice's animation, which runs asynchronously on a completely separate hook (`renderChatMessageHTML`) and takes roughly 2 seconds to finish. Code that does `await ChatMessage.create(...)` and then immediately reveals the roll's outcome — a `this.render()` that shows derived UI, an `actor.update()`/`item.update()` that changes a visible sheet field, a dialog that pops up next — will do so **before the dice have visually landed**, even though the code reads top-to-bottom as if it waited.

**The fix — `CONFIG.DODE.waitForDiceAnimation(message)`** (`scripts/helpers/config.mjs`): wraps DSN's own public API `game.dice3d.waitFor3DAnimationByMessageID(message.id)` in a `Promise.race(..., 4000ms timeout)`. `await` it between posting the message and doing anything that reveals the outcome. No-op if DSN isn't installed (`game.dice3d` is undefined).

**Why the 4s timeout is load-bearing, not decorative:** live-verified (2026-08-02) that the DSN promise **never resolves at all** while the browser tab is `document.hidden` — DSN's Three.js render loop is driven by `requestAnimationFrame`, which browsers fully suspend for backgrounded tabs. Without the race/timeout, a player who alt-tabs mid-roll would leave the wizard (or the sheet) stuck waiting forever. This was found empirically, not reasoned out in advance — trust the test, not the assumption that a promise from a well-behaved module always resolves.

**A companion bug worth checking for whenever you touch a roll:** several of the incidents above turned out to have an EVEN simpler problem underneath — the roll never went through `ChatMessage.create`/`Roll#toMessage()` at all (`#onRollSocialStanding`, `#onRollStartCapital`, the wizard's `#onRollFormaga` all just did `new Roll(...).evaluate()` and set state directly). No animation wait can fix a roll nobody ever saw. Before adding the wait, confirm the roll is actually posted to chat with `rolls:` attached.

**Where this has been applied (2026-08-02):** `character-wizard.mjs` — hjältedåd (both steps), svärdshand, socialt stånd, startkapital, särskild förmåga. `actor-character-sheet.mjs` — `#onRollAbility`. `rolls/fv-roll.mjs`'s `rollFV` — the core skill-roll engine used by `rollSkill`/`rollAttack`/`castSpell` — now waits **before** applying the EP-streck (`awardItemEp`) or returning control to a caller like `castSpell` that deducts PSY on the outcome; fixing it once here means every caller gets the fix for free without their own code changing. The confirm-roll (perfekt/fummel) was also missing from the animated `rolls` array — added alongside.

**Known follow-up, not yet touched:** `rolls/attack.mjs`'s `resolveAttack`/`postAttackCard` (SLB s.16-18 combat engine) has the same shape — `postAttackCard` receives an already-fully-resolved `result` (damage applied, EP already computed) and just displays it — but it's exposed only as a GM/macro API (`game.dode.resolveAttack(...)`), not wired to any sheet button yet. Apply the same pattern when it gets a UI, not before — no point taking on the risk of restructuring its side-effect ordering for code nobody can click through yet.

### Rule: v14-karnan slutar tillampa utgangna ActiveEffects sjalv — men raderar dem inte

⚠ **Testat 2026-07-29**, foranlett av ett Gemini-rad om att installera *Times Up*.
En AE med `duration.seconds: 3600` och `startTime`, efter `game.time.advance(7200)`:

| Falt | Efter utgang |
|---|---|
| `duration.remaining` | **-3600** (karnan raknar) |
| `effect.active` | **false** ⭐ |
| `actor.appliedEffects` | **0** |
| Andringen pa aktoren | **borta** (bonus tillbaka till 0) |
| Dokumentet finns kvar | **ja** — `disabled` ar fortfarande `false` |

**Slutsats:** for var del racker karnan. En tand fackla slutar verka nar tiden gar,
utan modul. *Times Up* tillfor **stadning** (att faktiskt radera de utgangna
dokumenten) och extra triggers — trevligt, inte nodvandigt.

⚠ **Notera skillnaden mellan `active` och `disabled`.** En utgangen effekt har
`active: false` men `disabled: false`. Kod som filtrerar pa `disabled` for att
avgora om en effekt galler **far fel svar** — filtrera pa `active` eller anvand
`actor.appliedEffects`.

⚠ **Det Gemini-radet missar:** en AE kan inte andra en tokens ljusutstralning.
AE-andringar traffar aktorens systemdata, medan ljus bor pa TokenDocument. En
fackla som faktiskt LYSER kraver antingen *Active Token Effects* eller egen kod som
slar pa/av tokenljuset nar effekten borjar och slutar. Se backlogpost 57.

### Rule: Scene#background is deprecated — Foundry v14 moved to a `levels`-collection, and writing the old field name silently does nothing on NEW scenes

⚠ **Upptäckt 2026-07-30**, byggandet av rollpersonsguidens scen. `Scene.create({ background: { src } })` skapade ett dokument där `background.src` var **`null`**, trots att alla andra fält i objektet (tint, anchorX, fit, …) fanns med sina defaultvärden — inget felmeddelande, inget kastat undantag.

Orsaken: i den installerade Foundry-versionen (v14 build 365) har `Scene` fått ett **`levels`**-fält (en `EmbeddedCollectionField` av "Level"-dokument, vart och ett med egen `background`/`foreground`/`fog`/`textures`), och det gamla toppnivåfältet `background` är kvar bara som en **läs-kompatibilitetsgetter** — konsolen loggar uttryckligen *"Scene#background is deprecated. Use Level#background and Level#textures instead."* när man LÄSER det. Getter fungerar för **befintliga** scener (som redan har ett riktigt `_source.background.src` från när de packades under en äldre modell), men vid **skapande av en ny scen** initieras i stället en `levels`-array med ett `defaultLevel0000`-element vars `background.src` är `null` — och att skriva till det gamla fältnamnet, vare sig vid `Scene.create()` eller via `scene.update({"background.src": ...})` i efterhand, går rakt förbi utan att röra `levels[0]`.

**Rätt sätt att sätta en ny scens bakgrund i den här Foundry-versionen:**

```js
const scene = await Scene.create({ name, width, height, grid: {...}, /* INTE background här */ });
const levelId = scene._source.levels[0]._id;   // "defaultLevel0000"
await scene.updateEmbeddedDocuments("Level", [{
  _id: levelId,
  "background.src": "systems/.../din-bild.png",
  "background.color": "#c9c4bb"
}]);
// Den gamla gettern `scene.background.src` läser nu rätt värde igen.
```

⚠ **Kontrollera alltid `Scene.schema.fields` i den faktiskt installerade versionen** innan man antar ett fältnamn — se den allmänna metodiken i "General methodology for is this Foundry behavior actually what I think it is?" ovan. `packs/scener/_source/*.json` (Utkanten, Värdshuset) är opåverkade eftersom de redan var packade med det gamla flata `background`-fältet innan denna upptäckt; **nya scen-JSON-filer som skrivs för hand i framtiden behöver `levels`-formen** för att renderas korrekt i den här Foundry-versionen.

### Rule: document IDs must be EXACTLY 16 alphanumeric characters

⚠ **Snubblat på två gånger** (embeddade `_key`-former 2026-07-28, scen-`_id` 2026-07-29).
Foundry accepterar `_id` som är kortare eller längre utan att klaga vid packning —
`fvtt package pack` rapporterar "Packed" som vanligt — men dokumentet kommer
tillbaka **med `id: null` och alla fält tomma** när det läses.

```js
"dodeVardshus01"    // 14 tecken → tyst trasigt
"dodeUtkanten01x"   // 15 tecken → tyst trasigt
"dodeVardshusUtk1"  // 16 tecken → fungerar
```

⚠ **Symptomet ser ut som något annat.** En scen med ogiltigt `_id` visar
`background.src === null`, vilket läses som en trasig bildsökväg — man letar i
`assets/` i stället för på nyckeln. Samma sak för items: tomma `system`-fält
ser ut som ett schemafel.

**Kontrollera alltid `_id.length === 16` innan packning.** För embeddade dokument
gäller det både förälderns och barnets id i `_key`:

| Embeddat | Nyckelform |
|---|---|
| Items på en aktör | `!actors.items!<actorId>.<itemId>` |
| Resultat i en RollTable | `!tables.results!<tableId>.<resultId>` |
| Sidor i en JournalEntry | `!journal.pages!<journalId>.<pageId>` |
| Mappar | `!folders!<id>` |

`foundry.utils.randomID()` ger rätt längd; handskrivna läsbara id:n gör det sällan.

### Rule: compendium visibility is per-PACK and role-based — there is no per-document hiding

Verified 2026-07-27. `CompendiumCollection#getUserLevel` (`foundry.mjs:28012`) computes a user's access to a pack by iterating **only** the pack's own `ownership` map (a `USER_ROLES` → `DOCUMENT_OWNERSHIP_LEVELS` object) and taking the highest matching role's level; `CompendiumCollection#testUserPermission` (`:28032`) consults nothing else beyond a `user.isGM → OWNER` short-circuit. **Individual documents inside a compendium have no independent visibility.** If a player can see the pack, they can see and read every document in it, and can drag any Item from it straight onto their own character sheet.

Consequences that must drive design, not be discovered later:

- **A pack is a visibility unit.** Split packs by *audience* first and topic second. "One mundane-equipment pack + one GM-only magic-item pack" is not redundancy — it is the only mechanism Foundry offers.
- **Packs are visible by default.** `CompendiumOwnershipField`'s initial value is `{PLAYER: "OBSERVER", ASSISTANT: "OWNER"}` (`:82171`). Omitting `ownership` from a `system.json`/`module.json` pack entry means *players can read it* — including adventure and monster content. This project shipped exactly that mistake until 2026-07-27.
- **The world overrides the manifest, and it sticks.** `core.compendiumConfiguration` (`:27669`) is a world setting that wins over the manifest for any pack a GM has configured by hand. A manifest fix therefore reaches *new* worlds and untouched packs, but will **not** retroactively repair a world where someone already set that pack's ownership manually. When debugging "why is this still visible", read that setting before re-reading the manifest.
- **Items are not Scenes.** A compendium Scene must be imported by a GM before it can be activated or viewed, which incidentally gatekeeps it. Items have no such step — pack visibility is the *only* gate on an Item. Do not assume the scene workflow generalises.

### Rule: a value derived from `game.time.worldTime` (or other globally-mutable state) must be a getter, not a `prepareDerivedData()`-assigned field

⚠ **Hittad i liveverifiering 2026-07-31** (backlog 7, "Runas Duntofflor"s tidsbegränsade aktivering). `prepareDerivedData()` skriver plana fält (`this.foo = ...`) som bara räknas om när Foundry faktiskt anropar `Document#prepareData()` — vilket sker vid dokumentets egna CRUD (`create`/`update`/`delete`, embedded eller ej), INTE när ett obesläktat globalt värde som `game.time.worldTime` ändras för sig självt (`game.time.advance()` utan att röra dokumentet). En bonus villkorad på `worldTime < activeUntil`, skriven som ett vanligt fält, fortsatte gälla for evigt tills något ANNAT råkade trigga en omräkning av just det dokumentet.

**Varför `ActiveEffect#duration`/`#active` inte har samma problem:** de är riktiga JS-getters på kärnklassen, beräknade vid VARJE läsning mot aktuell `game.time.worldTime` — se §6-regeln ovan om att v14-kärnan självmant slutar tillämpa en utgången effekt. `Actor#appliedEffects` filtrerar på `.active` och är därför också alltid färsk, utan att någon extern kod behöver trigga en omräkning.

**Fix:** gör motsvarande systemkod till en riktig `get`-accessor på DataModel-klassen (`actor-character.mjs`s `skillModifierTotals`/`skillModifierSources` är exemplet) i stället för ett fält satt i `prepareDerivedData()`. Regeln gäller allt som beror på `game.time.worldTime`, `game.user`, eller annat state som kan ändras utan att just DET dokumentet uppdateras.

### General methodology for "is this Foundry behavior actually what I think it is?"

1. Don't trust a code comment's explanation of Foundry internals at face value, even one that looks confident and cites a version number — verify it against the actual running Foundry build before building further logic on top of it (this project's now-fixed `apply()` override had exactly such a comment).
2. Grep `resources/app/public/scripts/foundry.mjs` for the method/property name in question. Search for its definition (`methodName(` at the start of a line, appropriately indented) and read the surrounding class, not just the first match.
3. If behavior is still unclear from source, write a throwaway `page.evaluate()` script via Playwright MCP against a temporary `Actor.create()`/`createEmbeddedDocuments()` in a real running world, log the result, then delete the actor. This project's bug hunt used exactly this technique to distinguish "throws when called directly" from "is never called by the real pipeline" — a distinction `node --check` and static reading cannot reveal.
4. Watch the browser console for `logCompatibilityWarning` output (format: `"... is deprecated. Please use X instead.", {since, until}`) — these name the exact replacement API and the version by which the old one stops working entirely.

---

## 7. Package, Content & Visibility Architecture

> Established 2026-07-27 after an audit found **every** compendium — including the campaign adventure pack and all monster stat blocks — readable by the world's Player-role account, and the two magic items sitting in the shoppable equipment pack at `price: 0` (free, unlimited, at character creation). Neither was a bug to patch; both were symptoms of having no package/visibility architecture. This section is the base that content and features are built on top of.

### 7.1 The two visibility layers

The single most important idea here: **visibility is not static.** A magic shop the party can browse for one town visit, loot they find mid-dungeon, an adventure that becomes relevant in session 4 — none of these can be expressed by compendium ownership alone (see §6: no per-document hiding, and toggling a whole pack would expose the entire catalogue at once). Two distinct layers are needed, and they do different jobs:

| | Layer 1 — static baseline | Layer 2 — dynamic, in-play |
|---|---|---|
| **Mechanism** | compendium pack `ownership` in the manifest | document `ownership` on **world** Actors/Items |
| **Granularity** | per pack, per role | per document, per user |
| **Changes** | set once, rarely revisited | constantly, by the GM, during play |
| **Answers** | "what may a player browse at any time?" | "what has the party found or been shown?" |

Layer 2 requires **no new code** — it is Foundry's native ownership model used as intended. What it requires is that the workflow be *written down* (below) instead of rediscovered each campaign.

### 7.1b Two claims worth getting right (both tested 2026-07-27)

Widely repeated summaries of Foundry's privacy model get one half right and one half wrong. Both halves were tested empirically in this world:

- ❌ **"Adventure compendiums are GM-only by default; players don't even see the title."** **False as an engine statement.** The default is `PLAYER: "OBSERVER"` (§6). Commercial adventure modules *look* like this because their authors declared `ownership` in the manifest — the engine does nothing for you. This project's own campaign module shipped without it and was fully readable by the Player account until 2026-07-27. Never rely on the default being safe.
- ✅ **"After importing, content is GM-private until revealed."** **True, and verified**: a freshly created world document gets `ownership = {default: 0}` (NONE) plus OWNER for its creator, and `testUserPermission(player, "LIMITED")` returns `false`. So Scenes, Actors and Journals imported from an adventure genuinely are GM-only until the GM activates the scene, grants ownership, or uses *Show to Players*. This is layer 2 (§7.1) working as designed — and it is the half you *can* rely on.

**Corollary for shipping a campaign module — assets do not travel with the import.** Importing an Adventure copies *documents* into the world, but every `img`/`background`/`texture` path still points into the module's own directory. Our `adventures` pack references 7 such paths (`modules/de-brutna-sigillens-kronika/assets/dimon/*.png`). Uninstalling or disabling the module after import therefore breaks every map and token image. Any player-facing install guide must say **keep the module installed and enabled**, contradicting the common "you can uninstall it afterwards, the content lives in the world now" advice.

### 7.2 The GM handoff workflows (layer 1 → layer 2)

All three follow the same shape: content lives hidden in a pack, the GM imports it into the world, ownership grants access.

- **Loot ("you find it")** — GM drags the item from the hidden pack directly onto the character sheet, or into a loot Actor the party holds `OBSERVER` on. The item becomes a world document the player owns.
- **Merchant / magic shop** — GM imports that shop's stock into a merchant NPC Actor (e.g. "Magiska boden i \<stad\>") and grants the party `OBSERVER` on that Actor while they're in town, then revokes it. **Only that shop's stock is ever exposed — never the whole magic catalogue.** This is why the answer is a merchant Actor and not "temporarily un-hide the magic pack".
- **Adventures** — already correct: the campaign module ships Foundry `Adventure` documents, a core type *designed* to be GM-imported into a world. Players never read the pack; they experience the imported result.
- **Lore / handouts (journals)** — ⚠ **"Show to Players" is a transient broadcast, not a permission grant.** Verified in source: `Journal.show()` (`foundry.mjs:44472`) only emits a `showEntry` socket event; it never writes `ownership`. Its own docstring states the document "will only be shown to players who have permission to observe it", and `force: true` merely displays it to everyone *at that moment*. So the common claim that Show-to-Players "updates the default permission to Observer so they can re-read it later" is **wrong** — close the popup and it's gone. To make a handout **persist** in a player's journal directory you must *also* set ownership (`OBSERVER`) via Configure Ownership. Use Show-to-Players for the dramatic reveal, ownership for the permanent record; they are independent, and a lasting handout needs both.

### 7.3 Three package tiers

| Tier | Package | Contains | Visibility posture |
|---|---|---|---|
| **System** | `drakar-och-demoner-expert` | rules engine + generic, non-spoiler rules content | player-visible **only** where character creation needs it |
| **Campaign module** | `de-brutna-sigillens-kronika` | adventure content, secret spells, environment effects, unique treasure | **GM-only, always** |
| **World** | the live world | instances actually in play + all of layer 2 | per-document |

**The rule, stated once:** *a pack is a visibility unit — if players must self-serve from it, it contains no secrets; everything else is GM-only and reaches players only through layer 2.*

A useful consequence: "should this secret spell go in the system's `besvarjelser` pack?" is answered by the tier boundary, not by taste. It's campaign content, so it goes in the campaign module.

### 7.4 Pack layout

| Pack | Package | `PLAYER` ownership | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| `raser`, `yrken` | system | `OBSERVER` | the wizard reads them during chargen |
| `besvarjelser` | system | `OBSERVER` | ordinary spells; magic-users need them during *and after* chargen |
| `vapen-utrustning` | system | `OBSERVER` | wizard equipment shop — **mundane items only** |
| `magiska-foremal` | system | `NONE` | magic/unique treasure; home for future found items |
| `monster` | system | `NONE` | stat blocks; nothing at chargen reads this pack |
| `adventures` | campaign module | `NONE` | spoilers; reaches play via Adventure import |
| `dimon-*` | world (`dode-test`) | `NONE` | temporary test packs |

### 7.5 Content registry — packages contribute, code doesn't hardcode

`character-wizard.mjs` originally hardcoded three pack IDs, which both blocked the pack reorganisation above and made it impossible for a campaign module to add a race, profession or item to character creation. Following dnd5e's `registry.mjs` precedent, `CONFIG.DODE.contentPacks` maps a semantic key to a **list** of pack IDs:

```js
DODE.contentPacks = {
  races: [...], professions: [...], startingEquipment: [...], spells: [...]
};
```

Design rules for it:

- A module extends chargen by pushing its own pack ID onto the relevant array in its `init` hook — no system change required.
- **Membership in this registry is the primary access guard**, not permission-filtering. `magiska-foremal` and `monster` are deliberately absent, so they can never reach the chargen UI *even for a GM* running the wizard. Permission checks alone would fail here, because a GM passes every permission check.
- Packs the current user cannot read are skipped at resolve time, so a hidden pack degrades gracefully rather than throwing.
- Defence in depth: the equipment step additionally excludes any item carrying ActiveEffects, so a magical item mis-filed into a shoppable pack still cannot be bought.

### 7.6 What Foundry does not provide (and what mature systems built instead)

Surveyed from the dnd5e and PF2e repositories 2026-07-27, to distinguish "we haven't built this yet" from "the engine genuinely doesn't do this". Useful both as validation and as a roadmap.

| They built | The Foundry gap | Relevance here |
|---|---|---|
| PF2e `rules/` (Rules Elements) | AE `change.key` cannot target an embedded Item | **Validates §3 backlog 7** — our skill-modifier blocker is a known engine limit, not a mistake in our design. A bespoke aggregation layer is the accepted answer. |
| PF2e `item/identification.ts` | no identified/unidentified concept in core | the "mysterious glowing sword" flow, if we want it |
| PF2e `apps/compendium-browser/` | core compendium UI is weak for *finding* content | the wizard is already a partial, chargen-shaped version of this |
| dnd5e `registry.mjs` | no content-registration API | adopted as §7.5 |
| both: `migration/` | core migrates *schema*; your world data is your problem | §3 backlog 3 (`niva` 3→4) is exactly this |
| PF2e `treasure/`, `container/`, `physical/` | no loot/container primitives | §7.2's handoff workflows |
| dnd5e advancement | no character-progression lifecycle | the wizard re-entry feature |
| PF2e `pick-a-thing-prompt.ts` | no generic "choose one" prompt | we built one ad-hoc in the skill picker; worth generalising if a third use appears |
